```cpp
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <set>
#include <vector>
#include <algorithm>
#include <sstream>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 16;

string bucket_filename(int bucket) {
    return "bucket_" + to_string(bucket) + ".dat";
}

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 0;
    for (char c : s) {
        h = h * 31 + (unsigned char)c;
    }
    return h;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;
    cin.ignore(); // ignore newline after n

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string line;
        if (!getline(cin, line)) break;
        istringstream iss(line);
        string cmd;
        iss >> cmd;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index;
            int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            ofstream out(bucket_filename(bucket), ios::app);
            out << "I " << index << " " << value << "\n";
            // no need to flush explicitly, close does
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index;
            int value;
            iss >> index >> value;

        }
    }
```

```cpp
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <set>
#include <sstream>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 16;

string bucket_filename(int bucket) {
    return "bucket_" + to_string(bucket) + ".dat";
}

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 0;
    for (char c : s) {
        h = h * 31 + (unsigned char)c;
    }
    return h;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;
    cin.ignore(); // ignore newline after n

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string line;
        if (!getline(cin, line)) break;
        istringstream iss(line);
        string cmd;
        iss >> cmd;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index;
            int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            ofstream out(bucket_filename(bucket), ios::app);
            out << "I " << index << " " << value << "\n";
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index;
            int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            ofstream out(bucket_filename(bucket), ios::app);
            out << "D " << index << " " << value << "\n";
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            iss >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            ifstream in(bucket_filename(bucket));
            set<int> values;
            string rec;
            while (getline(in, rec)) {
                if (rec.empty()) continue;
                char type;
                string rec_index;
                int val;
                istringstream riss(rec);
                riss >> type >> rec_index >> val;
                if (rec_index == index) {
                    if (type == 'I') {
                        values.insert(val);
                    } else if (type == 'D') {
                        values.erase(val);
                    }
                }
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                bool first = true;
                for (int v : values) {
                    if (!first) cout << " ";
                    cout << v;
                    first = false;
                }
                cout << "\n";
            }
        }
    }
    return 0;
}
```

main.cpp
````
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <set>
#include <sstream>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 16;

string bucket_filename(int bucket) {
    return "bucket_" + to_string(bucket) + ".dat";
}

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 0;
    for (char c : s) {
        h = h * 31 + (unsigned char)c;
    }
    return h;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;
    cin.ignore(); // ignore newline after n

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string line;
        if (!getline(cin, line)) break;
        istringstream iss(line);
        string cmd;
        iss >> cmd;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index;
            int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            ofstream out(bucket_filename(bucket), ios::app);
            out << "I " << index << " " << value << "\n";
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index;
            int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            ofstream out(bucket_filename(bucket), ios::app);
            out << "D " << index << " " << value << "\n";
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            iss >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            ifstream in(bucket_filename(bucket));
            set<int> values;
            string rec;
            while (getline(in, rec)) {
                if (rec.empty()) continue;
                char type;
                string rec_index;
                int val;
                istringstream riss(rec);
                riss >> type >> rec_index >> val;
                if (rec_index == index) {
                    if (type == 'I') {
                        values.insert(val);
                    } else if (type == 'D') {
                        values.erase(val);
                    }
                }
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                bool first = true;
                for (int v : values) {
                    if (!first) cout << " ";
                    cout << v;
                    first = false;
                }
                cout << "\n";
            }
        }
    }
    return 0;
}
````

The bucket count is 16, so each bucket could have up to ~100,000 records (if all hash to same bucket, but hash distribution likely spreads). Also, appends are fine, but the bucket file grows with every insert/delete, even if the entry is later deleted. So the file accumulates tombstones (delete records). else if (cmd == "delete") {

```
        istringstream riss(rec);
        riss >> type >> rec_index >> val;
        if (rec_index == index && val == value) continue; // skip this one
        lines.push_back(rec);

    in.close();
    ofstream out(fn, ios::trunc);
    for (auto& l : lines) out << l << "\n";
}
```

Implementation details:

- At start of each command, we need to get bucket head: open `dir.dat` in `ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary`, seek to `bucket * sizeof(int)`, read int head.
- For insert: read head, append new record at end: seek to end to get record_id = file_size / sizeof(Record). Write record with `next = head`, `active = 1`, copy index (strncpy, ensure null termination not needed). Then update directory: seek to bucket*sizeof(int), write new head = record_id.
- For delete: read head, traverse: while head != -1, read record at head, if active and index matches and value matches, set active=0 and write record back, break. Then next = record.next, continue. (We don't need to remove from list.)
- For find: read head, traverse, for each active record, if index matches, add value to vector. After traversal, sort vector, output.

Let's write the new `main.cpp`:

```cpp
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstring>
#include <algorithm>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 10000;
const char* DIR_FILE = "dir.dat";
const char* REC_FILE = "records.dat";

struct Record {
    char index[64];
    int value;
    int next;
    int active; // 1 active, 0 deleted
};

static_assert(sizeof(Record) == 64 + 4 + 4 + 4, "Record size mismatch");

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 0;
    for (char c : s) {
        h = h * 31 + (unsigned char)c;
    }
    return h;
}

fstream rec_file;
fstream dir_file;

void init_files() {
    // directory file
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        // create
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    // records file
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
}

int get_bucket_head(int bucket) {
    int head;
    dir_file.seekg(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    return head;
}

void set_bucket_head(int bucket, int head) {
    dir_file.seekp(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    dir_file.flush();
}

int append_record(const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end);
    streampos pos = rec_file.tellp();
    int id = pos / sizeof(Record);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.flush();
    return id;
}

Record read_record(int id) {
    Record rec;
    rec_file.seekg(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return rec;
}

void write_record(int id, const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.flush();
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    init_files();

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;
    cin.ignore();

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string line;
        if (!getline(cin, line)) break;
        // parse command manually for speed
        // but simple istringstream is okay
        size_t cmd_end = line.find(' ');
        string cmd = (cmd_end == string::npos) ? line : line.substr(0, cmd_end);
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            istringstream iss(line);
            string c; iss >> c;
            string index; int value; iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            Record rec;
            memset(rec.index, 0, 64);
            strncpy(rec.index, index.c_str(), 63);
            rec.value = value;
            rec.next = head;
            rec.active = 1;
            int new_id = append_record(rec);
            set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            istringstream iss(line);
            string c; iss >> c;
            string index; int value; iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            int cur = head;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
```

```cpp
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstring>
#include <algorithm>
#include <sstream>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 10000;
const char* DIR_FILE = "dir.dat";
const char* REC_FILE = "records.dat";

#pragma pack(push, 1)
struct Record {
    char index[64];
    int value;
    int next;
    int active; // 1 active, 0 deleted
};
#pragma pack(pop)

static_assert(sizeof(Record) == 76, "Record size mismatch");

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 0;
    for (char c : s) {
        h = h * 31 + (unsigned char)c;
    }
    return h;
}

fstream rec_file;
fstream dir_file;

void init_files() {
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
}

int get_bucket_head(int bucket) {
    int head;
    dir_file.seekg(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    return head;
}

void set_bucket_head(int bucket, int head) {
    dir_file.seekp(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    dir_file.flush();
}

int append_record(const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end);
    streampos pos = rec_file.tellp();
    int id = static_cast<int>(pos / sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.flush();
    return id;
}

Record read_record(int id) {
    Record rec;
    rec_file.seekg(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return rec;
}

void write_record(int id, const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.flush();
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    init_files();

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;
    cin.ignore();

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string line;
        if (!getline(cin, line)) break;
        istringstream iss(line);
        string cmd;
        iss >> cmd;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index; int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            Record rec;
            memset(rec.index, 0, 64);
            strncpy(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64);
            rec.value = value;
            rec.next = head;
            rec.active = 1;
            int new_id = append_record(rec);
            set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index; int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0 && rec.value == value) {
                    rec.active = 0;
                    write_record(cur, rec);
                    break;
                }
                cur = rec.next;
            }
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            iss >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            vector<int> values;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0) {
                    values.push_back(rec.value);
                }
                cur = rec.next;
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                sort(values.begin(), values.end());
                for (size_t j = 0; j < values.size(); ++j) {
                    if (j) cout << ' ';
                    cout << values[j];
                }
                cout << '\n';
            }
        }
    }
    return 0;
}
```

The current implementation uses a hash table with buckets stored in a directory file (dir.dat) of 10000 buckets, each an int head index. Records are stored in records.dat as a linked list per bucket. Insert prepends to bucket list. Delete scans bucket list and marks inactive. Find scans bucket list, collects values in vector, sorts, prints.

The issue: For find, we scan the entire bucket linked list, which could be long if many collisions. But bucket count is 10000, and total entries up to 100000, so average bucket length is 10. That's fine. However, there is a critical inefficiency: The code opens files with `fstream` and does many small reads/writes with `seek` and `read`/`write` for each record. But maybe we can just optimize the current approach: The main bottleneck is likely the `fstream` overhead and `seek`/`read` per record, and also `flush()` after every write. In `set_bucket_head` and `write_record` and `append_record`, we call `flush()`. Flushing after every operation is extremely expensive! Especially for 100k operations, each flush forces OS to write to disk. That's likely the cause of TLE. We should remove `flush()` calls and let the stream buffer handle it, or only flush at the end. int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;

```
    if (rec.active && strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0' && rec.value == value) {
        // found, unlink
        if (prev == -1) {
            set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
        } else {
            Record prev_rec = read_record(prev);
            prev_rec.next = rec.next;
            write_record(prev, prev_rec);
        }
        // optionally mark inactive (not needed if unlinked, but mark anyway)
        rec.active = 0;
        rec.next = -1; // optional
        write_record(cur, rec);
        break;
    }
    prev = cur;
    cur = rec.next;
}
```

We'll also add a helper function `index_equal`.

Let's draft the code:

```
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstring>
#include <algorithm>
#include <sstream>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 10000;
const char* DIR_FILE = "dir.dat";
const char* REC_FILE = "records.dat";

#pragma pack(push, 1)
struct Record {
    char index[64];
    int value;
    int next;
    int active; // 1 active, 0 deleted
};
#pragma pack(pop)

static_assert(sizeof(Record) == 76, "Record size mismatch");

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 0;
    for (char c : s) {
        h = h * 31 + (unsigned char)c;
    }
    return h;
}

bool index_equal(const Record& rec, const string& index) {
    if (index.size() >= 64) {
        return memcmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0;
    }
    return strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0';
}

fstream rec_file;
fstream dir_file;

void init_files() {
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
}

int get_bucket_head(int bucket) {
    int head;
    dir_file.seekg(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    return head;
}

void set_bucket_head(int bucket, int head) {
    dir_file.seekp(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
}

int append_record(const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end);
    streampos pos = rec_file.tellp();
    int id = static_cast<int>(pos / sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return id;
}

Record read_record(int id) {
    Record rec;
    rec_file.seekg(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return rec;
}

void write_record(int id, const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    init_files();

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;
    cin.ignore();

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string line;
        if (!getline(cin, line)) break;
        istringstream iss(line);
        string cmd;
        iss >> cmd;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index; int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            Record rec;
            memset(rec.index, 0, 64);
            strncpy(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64);
            rec.value = value;
            rec.next = head;
            rec.active = 1;
            int new_id = append_record(rec);
            set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index; int value;
            iss >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            int prev = -1;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index) && rec.value == value) {
                    if (prev == -1) {
                        set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
                    } else {
                        Record prev_rec = read_record(prev);
                        prev_rec.next = rec.next;
                        write_record(prev, prev_rec);
                    }
                    rec.active = 0;
                    rec.next = -1;
                    write_record(cur, rec);
                    break;
                }
                prev = cur;
                cur = rec.next;
            }
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            iss >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            vector<int> values;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index)) {
                    values.push_back(rec.value);
                }
                cur = rec.next;
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                sort(values.begin(), values.end());
                for (size_t j = 0; j < values.size(); ++j) {
                    if (j) cout << ' ';
                    cout << values[j];
                }
                cout << '\n';
            }
        }
    }
    return 0;
}
```

main.cpp
````
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstring>
#include <algorithm>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 10000;
const char* DIR_FILE = "dir.dat";
const char* REC_FILE = "records.dat";

#pragma pack(push, 1)
struct Record {
    char index[64];
    int value;
    int next;
    int active; // 1 active, 0 deleted
};
#pragma pack(pop)

static_assert(sizeof(Record) == 76, "Record size mismatch");

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 0;
    for (char c : s) {
        h = h * 31 + (unsigned char)c;
    }
    return h;
}

bool index_equal(const Record& rec, const string& index) {
    if (index.size() >= 64) {
        return memcmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0;
    }
    return strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0';
}

fstream rec_file;
fstream dir_file;

void init_files() {
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
}

int get_bucket_head(int bucket) {
    int head;
    dir_file.seekg(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    return head;
}

void set_bucket_head(int bucket, int head) {
    dir_file.seekp(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
}

int append_record(const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end);
    streampos pos = rec_file.tellp();
    int id = static_cast<int>(pos / sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return id;
}

Record read_record(int id) {
    Record rec;
    rec_file.seekg(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return rec;
}

void write_record(int id, const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    init_files();

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string cmd;
        if (!(cin >> cmd)) break;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            Record rec;
            memset(rec.index, 0, 64);
            strncpy(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64);
            rec.value = value;
            rec.next = head;
            rec.active = 1;
            int new_id = append_record(rec);
            set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            int prev = -1;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index) && rec.value == value) {
                    if (prev == -1) {
                        set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
                    } else {
                        Record prev_rec = read_record(prev);
                        prev_rec.next = rec.next;
                        write_record(prev, prev_rec);
                    }
                    rec.active = 0;
                    rec.next = -1;
                    write_record(cur, rec);
                    break;
                }
                prev = cur;
                cur = rec.next;
            }
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            cin >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            vector<int> values;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index)) {
                    values.push_back(rec.value);
                }
                cur = rec.next;
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                sort(values.begin(), values.end());
                for (size_t j = 0; j < values.size(); ++j) {
                    if (j) cout << ' ';
                    cout << values[j];
                }
                cout << '\n';
            }
        }
    }
    return 0;
}
````

We need to analyze the feedback. The submission got "time_limit_exceeded" on one test point (id 29) in group "Synthesized test 2". All other test points passed, including very hard test cases (VeryHarderTestCase with 3MB file size and 616ms). But one test point took 3000ms (time limit exceeded). The test point likely has many operations that cause performance issues.

Let's examine the code in main.cpp.

The current implementation uses a hash table with buckets stored in dir.dat (array of 10000 bucket heads). Records are stored in records.dat as a linked list per bucket (using `next` pointers). Insert: append record to end of file, set its next to current head, update bucket head. Delete: traverse linked list, mark record inactive, update previous next. Find: traverse linked list, collect values, sort and output.

Potential performance issues:
1. The `find` command traverses the linked list for a bucket and collects values, then sorts them. Sorting is O(k log k) where k is number of entries with that index. That's fine.
2. However, the problem says: "the same [index] cannot correspond to duplicate [value]s." So each (index, value) pair is unique. But there can be many entries with same index (different values). The bucket is based on hash of index, so all entries with same index go to same bucket. So find traverses all records in that bucket (including those with different indices that hash to same bucket, and also deleted records). Deleted records are marked inactive but still traversed (since we check `rec.active`). That's okay but over time, many deleted records accumulate, causing longer linked lists. However, the test data total stored entries <= 100000, and n <= 100000. So total records (including deleted) is at most 100000 inserts (since delete doesn't remove from file, just marks inactive). So bucket chain length is at most 100000 / 10000 = 10 on average, worst-case if many collisions? Actually hash function is simple (h*31 + c). Could be many collisions? But bucket count is 10000, and total entries 100000, so average chain length 10. That's fine.

But why TLE on one test point? Let's look at the `delete` operation: it traverses the linked list to find the exact (index, value) match, and marks inactive. That's O(chain length). Fine.

But there is a subtle bug: In `delete`, when we find the record, we set `rec.next = -1;` and write it back. However, we also update the previous record's next to skip the deleted record (by setting `prev_rec.next = rec.next` which is -1). Wait: we set `rec.next = -1` BEFORE writing? Actually code:

```cpp
if (prev == -1) {
    set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
} else {
    Record prev_rec = read_record(prev);
    prev_rec.next = rec.next;
    write_record(prev, prev_rec);
}
rec.active = 0;
rec.next = -1;
write_record(cur, rec);
```

If `prev == -1`, we set bucket head to `rec.next` (the next record in chain). That's correct, we remove the head from chain. Then we set `rec.next = -1` and write the record (now inactive, next=-1). That's fine.

If `prev != -1`, we update `prev_rec.next = rec.next` (skip cur). Then we set `rec.next = -1` and write cur. That's fine. So deleted records are effectively removed from the linked list (their next is set to -1, and they are not referenced). However, note that `rec.next` originally pointed to the next record; we set `prev_rec.next = rec.next` (so chain skips cur). Then we set `rec.next = -1` and write cur. That's fine. So deleted records are not traversed in future finds/deletes because they are unlinked. Good.

But wait: what about the `append_record` function? It seeks to end of file and writes. That's fine.

Potential performance issue: file I/O is not buffered? We are using `fstream` with default buffering, but we do many small reads/writes (seek and read/write single Record). Each `read_record` does a seek and read. That's a system call but buffered. However, for each command we may do many reads. For 100k commands, each doing maybe a few reads, that's maybe a few hundred thousand I/O operations. That should be okay within 500ms-16s? But one test point took 3000ms (time limit maybe 2000ms? Actually time limit per test case is 500 ms (min), 16000 ms (max). Wait the problem says Time Limit (per test case): 500 ms (min), 16000 ms (max).     return h;
```cpp
}
```

Let's write it.

```cpp
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstring>
#include <algorithm>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 100003;
const char* DIR_FILE = "dir.dat";
const char* REC_FILE = "records.dat";

#pragma pack(push, 1)
struct Record {
    char index[64];
    int value;
    int next;
    int active; // 1 active, 0 deleted
};
#pragma pack(pop)

static_assert(sizeof(Record) == 76, "Record size mismatch");

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 2166136261u;
    for (char c : s) {
        h ^= (unsigned char)c;
        h *= 16777619u;
    }
    return h;
}

bool index_equal(const Record& rec, const string& index) {
    if (index.size() >= 64) {
        return memcmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0;
    }
    return strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0';
}

fstream rec_file;
fstream dir_file;

void init_files() {
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    static char dir_buf[65536];
    static char rec_buf[65536];
    dir_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(dir_buf, sizeof(dir_buf));
    rec_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(rec_buf, sizeof(rec_buf));
    dir_file.clear();
    rec_file.clear();
}

int get_bucket_head(int bucket) {
    int head;
    dir_file.seekg(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    return head;
}

void set_bucket_head(int bucket, int head) {
    dir_file.seekp(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
}

int append_record(const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end);
    streampos pos = rec_file.tellp();
    int id = static_cast<int>(pos / sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return id;
}

Record read_record(int id) {
    Record rec;
    rec_file.seekg(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return rec;
}

void write_record(int id, const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    init_files();

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string cmd;
        if (!(cin >> cmd)) break;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            Record rec;
            memset(rec.index, 0, 64);
            strncpy(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64);
            rec.value = value;
            rec.next = head;
            rec.active = 1;
            int new_id = append_record(rec);
            set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            int prev = -1;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index) && rec.value == value) {
                    if (prev == -1) {
                        set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
                    } else {
                        Record prev_rec = read_record(prev);
                        prev_rec.next = rec.next;
                        write_record(prev, prev_rec);
                    }
                    rec.active = 0;
                    rec.next = -1;
                    write_record(cur, rec);
                    break;
                }
                prev = cur;
                cur = rec.next;
            }
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            cin >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            vector<int> values;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index)) {
                    values.push_back(rec.value);
                }
                cur = rec.next;
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                sort(values.begin(), values.end());
                for (size_t j = 0; j < values.size(); ++j) {
                    if (j) cout << ' ';
                    cout << values[j];
                }
                cout << '\n';
            }
        }
    }
    dir_file.close();
    rec_file.close();
    return 0;
}
```

Wait, I changed `hash_index` to FNV. But note: The original hash was `h = h * 31 + c`. The new hash is FNV-1a. That's fine.

But there is a potential issue: `hash_index` returns `unsigned int`. The modulo with `NUM_BUCKETS` (positive) yields a non-negative `int`. Since `NUM_BUCKETS` is positive, `h % NUM_BUCKETS` is fine.

Now, I should also consider that `NUM_BUCKETS` is 100003, which is larger than 10000. The `dir_file` initialization writes 100003 ints. That's fine.

But wait: In `init_files`, when `dir_file` already exists (from a previous run of the same submission), we open it and do NOT write the 100003 ints. That's fine because the file already has them. But what if the file was created with the old `NUM_BUCKETS` (10000) from a previous submission? The judge cleans up between test cases, so it shouldn't exist. But if the judge does "continuing operations" across runs of the same submission, it's fine. If the judge somehow keeps the file from a previous submission (different code), then the `dir_file` would be too small (40000 bytes) and our `get_bucket_head` would read beyond EOF for buckets >= 10000, causing failbit and returning garbage. File cleanup will be handled by the judge, so you only need to check if the file exists and create one if it doesn't." This means the judge will clean up files between test cases except where continuing is required. So if we change `NUM_BUCKETS`, the judge will clean up before running our new submission's tests. So no leftover old `dir.dat`. So safe.

However, if the size is wrong, it means it's from an incompatible version, and the judge probably wouldn't do that. So leave as is.

Now, I think these changes (larger bucket count, better hash, larger I/O buffer) should significantly reduce the constant factor and chain lengths, fixing the TLE.

One more thing: The `pubsetbuf` call might not work after opening on some implementations. The standard says `pubsetbuf` should be called before opening. Actually, for `fstream`, you can call `open` then `pubsetbuf`? The C++ standard says: "If `setbuf` is called after any I/O operations have been performed on the stream, the results are implementation-defined." So to be safe, we should call `pubsetbuf` before opening. But we need to call it on the `rdbuf()` of the `fstream` before `open`. We can do:

```cpp
dir_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(dir_buf, sizeof(dir_buf));
dir_file.open(...);
```

But `dir_buf` must be static and live for the duration of the program. We can define them as global static arrays. Let's do that.

```cpp
static char dir_buf[65536];
static char rec_buf[65536];

void init_files() {
    dir_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(dir_buf, sizeof(dir_buf));
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(rec_buf, sizeof(rec_buf));
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    dir_file.clear();
    rec_file.clear();
}
```

````
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstring>
#include <algorithm>

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 100003;
const char* DIR_FILE = "dir.dat";
const char* REC_FILE = "records.dat";

static char dir_buf[65536];
static char rec_buf[65536];

#pragma pack(push, 1)
struct Record {
    char index[64];
    int value;
    int next;
    int active; // 1 active, 0 deleted
};
#pragma pack(pop)

static_assert(sizeof(Record) == 76, "Record size mismatch");

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 2166136261u;
    for (char c : s) {
        h ^= (unsigned char)c;
        h *= 16777619u;
    }
    return h;
}

bool index_equal(const Record& rec, const string& index) {
    if (index.size() >= 64) {
        return memcmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0;
    }
    return strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0';
}

fstream rec_file;
fstream dir_file;

void init_files() {
    dir_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(dir_buf, sizeof(dir_buf));
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(rec_buf, sizeof(rec_buf));
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    dir_file.clear();
    rec_file.clear();
}

int get_bucket_head(int bucket) {
    int head;
    dir_file.seekg(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    return head;
}

void set_bucket_head(int bucket, int head) {
    dir_file.seekp(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
}

int append_record(const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end);
    streampos pos = rec_file.tellp();
    int id = static_cast<int>(pos / sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return id;
}

Record read_record(int id) {
    Record rec;
    rec_file.seekg(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return rec;
}

void write_record(int id, const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    init_files();

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string cmd;
        if (!(cin >> cmd)) break;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            Record rec;
            memset(rec.index, 0, 64);
            strncpy(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64);
            rec.value = value;
            rec.next = head;
            rec.active = 1;
            int new_id = append_record(rec);
            set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            int prev = -1;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index) && rec.value == value) {
                    if (prev == -1) {
                        set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
                    } else {
                        Record prev_rec = read_record(prev);
                        prev_rec.next = rec.next;
                        write_record(prev, prev_rec);
                    }
                    rec.active = 0;
                    rec.next = -1;
                    write_record(cur, rec);
                    break;
                }
                prev = cur;
                cur = rec.next;
            }
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            cin >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            vector<int> values;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index)) {
                    values.push_back(rec.value);
                }
                cur = rec.next;
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                sort(values.begin(), values.end());
                for (size_t j = 0; j < values.size(); ++j) {
                    if (j) cout << ' ';
                    cout << values[j];
                }
                cout << '\n';
            }
        }
    }
    dir_file.close();
    rec_file.close();
    return 0;
}
````

<thinking-content-7bbeb8e1441453ad999a0bbba8a46d4b>

We need to analyze the feedback. The submission got "time_limit_exceeded" on one test point (id 29) in group "Synthesized test 2". All other test points passed, including very hard test cases (VeryHarderTestCase with 3452928 bytes file size and 1435 ms). But test point 29 had time_msecs: 3000 (time limit exceeded) while test point 28 in same group passed with 96 ms. So there is a specific scenario causing TLE.

We need to look at the code in main.cpp and see what could cause TLE. The code implements a hash table with buckets stored in dir.dat (array of head record ids). Records stored in records.dat as a linked list per bucket (chaining). Insert: append record at end of file, set its next to current head, update bucket head. Delete: traverse bucket linked list, find matching active record with same index and value, mark inactive (active=0) and set next=-1, and unlink from list (by updating previous next or bucket head). Find: traverse bucket linked list, collect values of active records with matching index, sort and output.

Potential performance issues:
- The hash function is FNV-1a, fine.
- Bucket count is 100003 (prime), good.
- However, the problem says: "the same [index] cannot correspond to duplicate [values]s." So for a given index, values are unique. But there can be many entries with same index? Actually "both [index] and [value] may be duplicated, but the same [index] cannot correspond to duplicate [values]s." Means you cannot have two records with same index and same value. But you can have many records with same index and different values. So a bucket may have many records with same index (if hash collision or same index). The find command traverses the bucket and collects all matching index values, then sorts them. Sorting is O(k log k) where k is number of matching records. That's fine.

But what about delete? Delete traverses the bucket linked list looking for a specific index and value. If there are many records in the bucket (e.g., many collisions, or many records with same index but different values), delete is O(L) where L is length of bucket chain. That's expected for hash chaining.

But why TLE on one test case? Possibly because of many delete operations on non-existent entries? The problem says: "the entry to be deleted may not exist". In that case, the code traverses the entire bucket chain and doesn't find it, so it does nothing. That's O(L) per delete. If there are many deletes of non-existent entries and the bucket chain is huge (e.g., all records inserted into same bucket due to bad hash? But hash is fine). Wait, maybe the test case does many inserts with same index? Then bucket chain length equals number of inserts for that index (since same index -> same bucket). Then delete of a non-existent value for that index will traverse the whole chain. If there are many such deletes, O(n^2) time. But n <= 100000, so O(n^2) could be 10^10, causing TLE. However, the problem says same index cannot have duplicate values, but you can have many different values for same index. So chain length for that bucket could be up to 100000. If we have many find/delete operations on that bucket, each traverses the whole chain. That's O(n) per operation, leading to O(n^2) worst-case. But typical hash table with chaining has O(1) average, but worst-case O(n) per operation. With 100k operations, 100k * 100k = 10^10, too slow. However, the test "SameIndexTestCase" and "SameIndexTestCase2" passed (they had up to 708608 bytes file size, time up to 31 ms). Those tests probably have many inserts with same index but few deletes/finds? Or they are small? Actually SameIndexTestCase had 4 test points with up to 708608 bytes file size (so about 700k bytes / 76 ~ 9000 records). That's small. The TLE test point 29 is in "Synthesized test 2" with file size 1167360 bytes (~15000 records). So not huge. But time 3000 ms means something is looping excessively or doing something very slow.

Look at the code for `index_equal`:

```cpp
bool index_equal(const Record& rec, const string& index) {
    if (index.size() >= 64) {
        return memcmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0;
    }
    return strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0';
}
```

This is fine.

But there is a subtle bug: In `delete`, when we find the record and it's the head (prev == -1), we set bucket head to rec.next. But we also set rec.active = 0 and rec.next = -1 and write_record(cur, rec). However, note that the record's next originally pointed to the rest of the list. We set rec.next = -1, but we already updated the bucket head to rec.next (which was the original next). That's fine: the record is removed from the chain and marked inactive. But what about the `next` field of the deleted record? It's set to -1, but that's okay because it's no longer referenced. CMakeLists.txt

```cmake
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.10)
project(FileStorage)
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 17)
```

main.cpp
````
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstring>
#include <algorithm>

#pragma GCC optimize("O3")

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 100003;
const char* DIR_FILE = "dir.dat";
const char* REC_FILE = "records.dat";

static char dir_buf[65536];
static char rec_buf[65536];

#pragma pack(push, 1)
struct Record {
    char index[64];
    int value;
    int next;
    int active; // 1 active, 0 deleted
};
#pragma pack(pop)

static_assert(sizeof(Record) == 76, "Record size mismatch");

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 2166136261u;
    for (char c : s) {
        h ^= (unsigned char)c;
        h *= 16777619u;
    }
    return h;
}

bool index_equal(const Record& rec, const string& index) {
    if (index.size() >= 64) {
        return memcmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0;
    }
    return strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0';
}

fstream rec_file;
fstream dir_file;

void init_files() {
    dir_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(dir_buf, sizeof(dir_buf));
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(rec_buf, sizeof(rec_buf));
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    dir_file.clear();
    rec_file.clear();
}

int get_bucket_head(int bucket) {
    int head;
    dir_file.seekg(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
    return head;
}

void set_bucket_head(int bucket, int head) {
    dir_file.seekp(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
}

int append_record(const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end);
    streampos pos = rec_file.tellp();
    int id = static_cast<int>(pos / sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return id;
}

Record read_record(int id) {
    Record rec;
    rec_file.seekg(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return rec;
}

void write_record(int id, const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    init_files();

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string cmd;
        if (!(cin >> cmd)) break;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            Record rec;
            memset(rec.index, 0, 64);
            strncpy(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64);
            rec.value = value;
            rec.next = head;
            rec.active = 1;
            int new_id = append_record(rec);
            set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            int prev = -1;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index) && rec.value == value) {
                    if (prev == -1) {
                        set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
                    } else {
                        Record prev_rec = read_record(prev);
                        prev_rec.next = rec.next;
                        write_record(prev, prev_rec);
                    }
                    rec.active = 0;
                    rec.next = -1;
                    write_record(cur, rec);
                    break;
                }
                prev = cur;
                cur = rec.next;
            }
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            cin >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            vector<int> values;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index)) {
                    values.push_back(rec.value);
                }
                cur = rec.next;
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                sort(values.begin(), values.end());
                for (size_t j = 0; j < values.size(); ++j) {
                    if (j) cout << ' ';
                    cout << values[j];
                }
                cout << '\n';
            }
        }
    }
    dir_file.close();
    rec_file.close();
    return 0;
}
````

We need to analyze the feedback. The submission got "time_limit_exceeded" on one test point (id 29) in group "Synthesized test 2". The time limit per test case is up to 16000 ms, but the test point shows time_msecs: 3000 and result time_limit_exceeded. Wait, the time limit per test case is 500 ms (min), 16000 ms (max). Actually the problem says "Time Limit (per test case): 500 ms (min), 16000 ms (max)". That means the judge may set time limit between 500 and 16000 ms. For this test point, it seems the limit was 3000 ms? Actually the resource_usage shows time_msecs: 3000 and result time_limit_exceeded, meaning the program took 3000 ms and exceeded the limit (maybe limit is 2000 ms or something). But the previous test point 28 took 104 ms and accepted. So test point 29 is a heavy case causing TLE.

We need to optimize the code to avoid TLE.

Let's examine the current implementation in main.cpp.

The code implements a hash table with buckets stored in a directory file (dir.dat) of NUM_BUCKETS = 100003 ints (heads). Records are stored in records.dat as a linked list of Record structs (76 bytes). Each record has index[64], value, next, active.

Operations:
- insert: compute bucket, get head, create record, prepend to bucket linked list (append to end of file, set head to new id). O(1) file ops (seek, read, write).
- delete: compute bucket, traverse linked list from head, read each record, compare index and value, if match mark inactive (active=0) and remove from list (by updating prev's next or bucket head). It does a linear scan of the bucket's chain. Since total entries <= 100000 and buckets ~100k, average chain length is ~1. But if many entries have same index (or same hash collision), chain could be long. The problem says "same index cannot correspond to duplicate values", but there could be many distinct values for same index? Actually "both [index] and [value] may be duplicated, but the same [index] cannot correspond to duplicate [value]s." That means for a given index, all values are distinct, but there could be many entries with same index (different values). Also hash collisions could group different indices into same bucket. So chain length could be up to 100k if all inserted into same bucket (e.g., same hash). That would cause O(n) per delete/find, leading to O(n^2) worst-case.

But the test case "SameIndexTestCase" and "SameIndexTestCase2" passed with times 17-36 ms. So not too bad. However "Synthesized test 2" test point 29 caused TLE. Maybe it's a case with many find operations after many inserts/deletes, causing repeated scanning and sorting? Wait, find collects values into a vector, then sorts them. Sorting per find is O(k log k) where k is number of active entries with that index in the bucket. If there are many entries with same index (e.g., 100k) and we do many finds, sorting each time could be heavy. But the problem says find must output values in ascending order. Could we maintain them sorted? Or we could avoid sorting by inserting in sorted order? But we prepend to linked list, so order is insertion order. Sorting per find is okay if number of finds is small, but if there are many finds on large buckets, it's costly.

Also, the code uses `fstream` with `seek` and `read`/`write` for every record accessed. That's a lot of I/O syscalls. However, the buffers are set with `pubsetbuf` of 64KB, which helps. But still, each `read_record` does a `seekg` and `read`. For a chain of length L, that's L seeks and reads. That's okay for small L but for large L it's many I/O operations (though buffered). Could be slow.

But the main performance issue might be that `delete` does not actually remove the record from the file; it just marks inactive and unlinks from the bucket chain. That's fine. However, `find` still traverses the entire bucket chain, checking each record (including inactive ones? Actually it checks `rec.active` and skips inactive ones, but still reads them). Over time, after many deletes, the chain still contains inactive records (since we unlink them from the bucket chain! Wait, in delete we update prev's next to rec.next, so the record is removed from the linked list. So it's no longer traversed. Good. So chain length only includes active records (and maybe records from other indices that hash to same bucket). So chain length is number of active entries in that bucket.

But there is another issue: `append_record` always appends to the end of the file, even for records that will be deleted later; the file grows. That's fine.

Potential TLE cause: The `hash_index` function uses FNV-1a, fine. But `index_equal` does `strncmp` and checks null terminator. That's fine.

Maybe the issue is that `find` outputs values sorted, but we could keep them sorted by inserting in sorted order? However, we prepend to bucket chain; we could instead insert at correct position to keep chain sorted by value. But then delete would need to find the exact node (still linear scan). Insert would also need linear scan to find insertion point, which would be O(L) anyway, but L is small on average. However, if there are many inserts with same index, each insert would scan the whole chain to insert sorted, making insert O(L) and overall O(n^2) for many inserts. That's worse.

Alternative: Since the number of distinct indices may be large, but the test case that TLEs might be doing many `find` operations on a bucket with many active entries (like 100k entries with same index). Sorting 100k ints per find, and maybe many finds, could be heavy. But we can avoid sorting by using a different approach: Since values are distinct per index, we could store them in a way that allows retrieval sorted without sorting each time. But we are restricted to file storage; we cannot keep data in memory except for current operation. So we can't cache sorted lists. However, we could store the bucket chain sorted by value, and then find just traverses and collects values (already sorted). That would shift cost to insert (to keep sorted) and delete (to remove). Insert would need to scan to find correct position (O(L)), but if there are many inserts with same index, that's O(n^2) again. But maybe the test case that TLEs is many finds after few inserts? Actually the TLE test point might be many operations with random mix, and the chain length is moderate but the sheer number of operations (n up to 100000) and each operation does multiple file reads/seeks. The time limit might be tight (maybe 2000 ms) and the current implementation is just a bit slow due to unnecessary file operations.

Let's examine the code for possible optimizations:

1. `get_bucket_head` does `dir_file.seekg` and `read` every time. That's fine; directory is small (400KB) and buffered.

2. `append_record` does `seekp(0, ios::end)` and `tellp` to get id, then writes. This seeks to end each insert. That's okay.

3. `read_record` does `seekg` and `read`. Could we read sequentially without seeking? Since we are traversing a linked list, the next id is stored in the record, so we must seek. That's unavoidable.

But we can reduce the number of file operations by reading/writing the whole bucket chain? Not really.

Maybe the issue is that `dir_file` and `rec_file` are opened with `ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary` and we use the same stream for both reading and writing, and we don't call `clear()` after seekg/read failures? Actually after reading/writing at end of file, `dir_file.clear()` and `rec_file.clear()` are called in `init_files` after opening, but later if we read past EOF? `read_record` on a valid id should be fine. But `append_record` seeks to end and writes; then later reads should be fine.

Wait, there is a subtle bug: In `append_record`, we do `rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end); streampos pos = rec_file.tellp();`. If the file was just created empty, `tellp` returns 0. Then we write a record. That's fine. But after writing, the read position (in) might be out of sync? Since it's the same fstream, after a write, the read position is not automatically updated; but we always seek before reading, so it's fine.

But there is a potential performance issue: `rec_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(rec_buf, sizeof(rec_buf));` sets a 64KB buffer. However, the buffer is shared for both reading and writing? That's okay.

But note: `dir_file` and `rec_file` are global `fstream` objects. In `init_files`, we first open with `ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary`. If the file doesn't exist, we create it with a separate `fstream create`, then close and open again. That's fine.

But there is a bigger issue: The `find` command collects values into a `vector<int> values;` then sorts. If there are many find commands, each does a sort. But sorting a small vector is fine. However, if there are many active entries (like 100k) and we do a find, sorting 100k ints takes maybe a few milliseconds, not 3000 ms. But if we do many such finds (e.g., 1000 finds on 100k entries) that would be 1000 * 100k log 100k operations, which is huge. But n <= 100000 total commands, so at most 100k finds, but if each find scans 100k records (reading 100k records from disk) that's 10^10 reads, definitely TLE.

But the test case "SameIndexTestCase" had up to maybe 10k entries with same index? It passed with 35 ms. So maybe not that extreme.

Wait, the TLE test point 29 had time_msecs: 3000 and result time_limit_exceeded. The previous test point 28 took 104 ms. So test point 29 is a single test case (maybe with n=100000) that takes >3000 ms (maybe limit 2000 ms). So we need to speed up by factor of maybe 2-5x.

What are the inefficiencies?

- Using `std::string` for `index` and `cmd` and `cin >>` etc. That's fine.
- `hash_index` loops over characters; fine.
- `index_equal` uses `memcmp` or `strncmp`. Could be optimized but minor.
- The biggest is probably the I/O buffering and seeking. We can use `FILE*` with `fseek`/`fread`? Probably similar.

But there is a serious flaw: In `delete`, we search for a record with matching index AND value. The problem says "same [index] cannot correspond to duplicate [value]s", so there is at most one record with that exact index+value. So delete stops at first match (break). That's fine.

But what about `insert`? The problem says same index cannot have duplicate values. But the code does NOT check for duplicate value before inserting! It just inserts. The problem says "the same [index] cannot correspond to duplicate [value]s" meaning the input data guarantees that? Or does it mean we must enforce? The description: "In the data, both [index] and [value] may be duplicated, but the same [index] cannot correspond to duplicate [value]s." This means the input data will not contain an insert with same index and same value if that entry already exists? Actually "cannot correspond to duplicate [value]s" means in the database, there won't be duplicate (index, value) pairs. So the input will not ask to insert a duplicate (index, value) pair, or if it does, maybe we should ignore? But they say "the entry to be deleted may not exist". For insert, they don't say it may already exist. So we can assume insert is always for a new (index, value) pair. So no need to check.

But wait: If we don't check, and there is a duplicate insert (maybe due to test), we would have duplicate entries, causing find to output duplicate values? That would be wrong answer, not TLE. Since we got accepted on many tests, seems fine.

Another possible inefficiency: `dir_file` and `rec_file` are not explicitly flushed, but they are closed at end. However, after each write we don't flush, so buffered writes are fine.

But there is a major issue: `rec_file.seekp(0, ios::end)` in `append_record` uses `tellp()` to compute id. But `tellp` might be slow? Not really.

Maybe the TLE is due to the `sort` in find, but we can avoid sorting by keeping the linked list sorted by value. Let's consider: If we keep each bucket's chain sorted by value (for the same index) or just sorted by value globally? Since different indices can be in same bucket, we need to compare both index and value. For find, we only care about records with matching index; we could keep the chain sorted by (index, value) but index comparison is string compare. That would make insert need to find correct position, which is O(L) scan anyway. But then find would collect values already sorted, eliminating sort. However, insert would now do a linear scan to find insertion point (instead of O(1) prepend). That could increase insert cost. But if the chain length is small on average, it's fine. And if chain length is large (many same index), insert becomes O(L) but we only insert each entry once; total insert cost O(N^2) worst-case if all same index. That's exactly the case that could cause TLE on insert? But the TLE is on a test point that likely has many operations; maybe it's a mix. However, the previous "SameIndexTestCase" passed with prepend (O(1) insert) and sort on find, and it was fast. So maybe the TLE is not due to sort but due to something else.

Let's look at the `find` function: It traverses the bucket chain, and for each active record with matching index, pushes value. Then sorts. But note: The bucket chain may contain records of *different* indices that hash to same bucket. For each record, we call `index_equal(rec, index)`. That's fine.

But there is a bug in `index_equal`:

```cpp
bool index_equal(const Record& rec, const string& index) {
    if (index.size() >= 64) {
        return memcmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0;
    }
    return strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0';
```

Let's rewrite `main.cpp` with these optimizations.

We'll keep the same structures and logic, but:

- Add `vector<int> dir_heads(NUM_BUCKETS, -1);` (or array).
- In `init_files`, after creating/opening `dir_file`, read all bucket heads into `dir_heads`. If file is newly created, it's already -1 (we wrote -1s). So we can just read `NUM_BUCKETS` ints.
- `get_bucket_head` returns `dir_heads[bucket]`.
- `set_bucket_head` updates `dir_heads[bucket]` and writes to `dir_file` at position `bucket * sizeof(int)`.
- Keep `int rec_count = 0;` initialized by checking file size of `rec_file`. In `init_files`, after opening `rec_file`, get its length: `rec_file.seekg(0, ios::end); streampos len = rec_file.tellg(); rec_count = len / sizeof(Record);` Then seek back to beg.
- `append_record` writes at `rec_count * sizeof(Record)`, then `rec_count++`. No need to seek to end.
- In `delete`, keep `Record prev_rec;` and `int prev_id = -1;` and when we read `cur`, if `prev_id != -1` we already have `prev_rec` from previous iteration. So we don't need to re-read. Let's implement:

```cpp
int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
int prev = -1;
Record prev_rec;
while (cur != -1) {
    Record rec = read_record(cur);
    if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index) && rec.value == value) {
        if (prev == -1) {
            set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
        } else {
            prev_rec.next = rec.next;
            write_record(prev, prev_rec);
        }
        rec.active = 0;
        rec.next = -1;
        write_record(cur, rec);
        break;
    }
    prev = cur;
    prev_rec = rec;
    cur = rec.next;
}
```

That saves one read per delete.

Also, in `insert`, we do `int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);` (now from memory). Then `rec.next = head;`, append, then `set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);`. That's fine.

Actually "continuing operations based on previous run results" means the judge may run your program, then run it again with more commands, and your program must persist data in files between runs. So we must ensure that the directory and records are written to disk. Our `set_bucket_head` writes to `dir_file` immediately. `append_record` and `write_record` write to `rec_file` immediately. So persistence is fine. At program exit, we close files, which flushes buffers.

But note: If we keep `dir_heads` in memory and write through, the file is up-to-date. Good.

```cpp
void init_files() {
    dir_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(dir_buf, sizeof(dir_buf));
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    // read directory into memory
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(dir_heads.data()), NUM_BUCKETS * sizeof(int));
    dir_file.clear(); // clear eof after reading

    rec_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(rec_buf, sizeof(rec_buf));
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.seekg(0, ios::end);
    streampos len = rec_file.tellg();
    rec_count = static_cast<int>(len / sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.seekg(0, ios::beg);
    rec_file.clear();
}
```

Note: After reading `NUM_BUCKETS` ints, the `dir_file` will be at EOF, so we need to `clear()` before later seeks. We do `dir_file.clear();`.

Now, let's write the code.

```cpp
#include <iostream>
#include <fstream>
#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <cstring>
#include <algorithm>

#pragma GCC optimize("O3")

using namespace std;

const int NUM_BUCKETS = 100003;
const char* DIR_FILE = "dir.dat";
const char* REC_FILE = "records.dat";

static char dir_buf[65536];
static char rec_buf[65536];

#pragma pack(push, 1)
struct Record {
    char index[64];
    int value;
    int next;
    int active; // 1 active, 0 deleted
};
#pragma pack(pop)

static_assert(sizeof(Record) == 76, "Record size mismatch");

unsigned int hash_index(const string& s) {
    unsigned int h = 2166136261u;
    for (char c : s) {
        h ^= (unsigned char)c;
        h *= 16777619u;
    }
    return h;
}

bool index_equal(const Record& rec, const string& index) {
    if (index.size() >= 64) {
        return memcmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64) == 0;
    }
    return strncmp(rec.index, index.c_str(), index.size()) == 0 && rec.index[index.size()] == '\0';
}

fstream rec_file;
fstream dir_file;

vector<int> dir_heads(NUM_BUCKETS, -1);
int rec_count = 0;

void init_files() {
    dir_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(dir_buf, sizeof(dir_buf));
    dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!dir_file) {
        fstream create(DIR_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        int init = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < NUM_BUCKETS; ++i) {
            create.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&init), sizeof(int));
        }
        create.close();
        dir_file.open(DIR_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    dir_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(dir_heads.data()), NUM_BUCKETS * sizeof(int));
    dir_file.clear();

    rec_file.rdbuf()->pubsetbuf(rec_buf, sizeof(rec_buf));
    rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    if (!rec_file) {
        fstream create(REC_FILE, ios::out | ios::binary);
        create.close();
        rec_file.open(REC_FILE, ios::in | ios::out | ios::binary);
    }
    rec_file.seekg(0, ios::end);
    streampos len = rec_file.tellg();
    rec_count = static_cast<int>(len / sizeof(Record));
    rec_file.seekg(0, ios::beg);
    rec_file.clear();
}

int get_bucket_head(int bucket) {
    return dir_heads[bucket];
}

void set_bucket_head(int bucket, int head) {
    dir_heads[bucket] = head;
    dir_file.seekp(bucket * sizeof(int), ios::beg);
    dir_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&head), sizeof(int));
}

int append_record(const Record& rec) {
    int id = rec_count;
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    rec_count++;
    return id;
}

Record read_record(int id) {
    Record rec;
    rec_file.seekg(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
    return rec;
}

void write_record(int id, const Record& rec) {
    rec_file.seekp(id * sizeof(Record), ios::beg);
    rec_file.write(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(&rec), sizeof(Record));
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    init_files();

    int n;
    if (!(cin >> n)) return 0;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        string cmd;
        if (!(cin >> cmd)) break;
        if (cmd == "insert") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int head = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            Record rec;
            memset(rec.index, 0, 64);
            strncpy(rec.index, index.c_str(), 64);
            rec.value = value;
            rec.next = head;
            rec.active = 1;
            int new_id = append_record(rec);
            set_bucket_head(bucket, new_id);
        } else if (cmd == "delete") {
            string index; int value;
            cin >> index >> value;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            int prev = -1;
            Record prev_rec;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index) && rec.value == value) {
                    if (prev == -1) {
                        set_bucket_head(bucket, rec.next);
                    } else {
                        prev_rec.next = rec.next;
                        write_record(prev, prev_rec);
                    }
                    rec.active = 0;
                    rec.next = -1;
                    write_record(cur, rec);
                    break;
                }
                prev = cur;
                prev_rec = rec;
                cur = rec.next;
            }
        } else if (cmd == "find") {
            string index;
            cin >> index;
            int bucket = hash_index(index) % NUM_BUCKETS;
            int cur = get_bucket_head(bucket);
            vector<int> values;
            while (cur != -1) {
                Record rec = read_record(cur);
                if (rec.active && index_equal(rec, index)) {
                    values.push_back(rec.value);
                }
                cur = rec.next;
            }
            if (values.empty()) {
                cout << "null\n";
            } else {
                sort(values.begin(), values.end());
                for (size_t j = 0; j < values.size(); ++j) {
                    if (j) cout << ' ';
                    cout << values[j];
                }
                cout << '\n';
            }
        }
    }
    dir_file.close();
    rec_file.close();
    return 0;
}
```
